ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e7391	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Values Advisors! Yes, there are great challenges that we have to face, and these challenges require the Federal Government to turn on the big screws, turn on the very large screws, and we are doing so in the form of three anti-trusion packages that we have already decided here in this House. Of course, high inflation continues to create major challenges. That is, I think, clear to everyone in this room and also at home. The rising costs in everyday life are of great concern to most people, which is no question at all, concerns which we, as politicians, must take very, very seriously. In the last few days and weeks, I am also aware that all of you, as we are sitting here, are taking these concerns seriously, but access to these issues may be somewhat different. This is, in principle, quite all right. Before discussing possible measures, one might also need to look at the causes of the level of inflation if one is serious. There are basically three reasons for this. [[Abg. Worm: ECB!]] – I know that there is still a fourth reason on the part of the liberals, but essentially there are three reasons. Firstly, the overheating of the economy after the corona pandemic when we had extremely strong growth phases, 5.3 percent were forecast. This has led to overheating of the economy. The economy has grown strongly, has recovered very strongly, of course, also because of the comprehensive covid aid. Secondly, this is a very up-to-date reason: the global bottlenecks that we feel due to the interrupted supply chains. [[Abg. Houses: Because of the Corona policy! Everything itself is caused by bad politics!]] Who would have thought that a cross-stand ship in the Sues Canal or a closed port in Shanghai had such an impact on supply chains and on the economy as a whole? And thirdly, The most up-to-date reason, of course, is the war in Ukraine, and especially the increased energy costs. As far as inflation is concerned, we have a situation different from that of the USA. The US has a much broader inflation. Our inflation in Europe is very strong in terms of energy prices, more than 50 percent, and the war in Ukraine has, of course, very massive consequences. What can the state do about inflation? Of course, since the ECB, yes, you are right, you have certain possibilities, the central banks as a whole. What can a state do? – A state cannot dispel 100 percent of all crises in this world. He cannot do that, and this is not the task of the state; but our task is to help people at this difficult stage with high prices and to best dispel these effects on prices. This is the task of politics, and we are doing so in a scale – Mr Klubobmann has mentioned it – and also at a speed with which we Europe, I would almost say, are not unique, but very, very, very forward in terms of volume, but above all – and that is crucial – as far as speed is concerned. In other countries it is currently being discussed whether certain measures are being debated in Parliament in autumn. We have already decided most of these measures. Yesterday, there was the announcement in Germany that the eco-electric fee, i.e. the EEG charge, will now be abolished with us eco-current rate and eco-current transport contribution. With us this has already been decided in the high house in spring. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] These are differences that you just have to take note of. As far as speed is concerned, we are on the front line, and I believe that this is also good and important in such a way that Parliament has reacted so quickly and so intensively. [[Abg. Anger: And what influence do the measures have on prices and inflation?]] It's not enough to just turn up the money tap. You have to look at this a little more serious. Many ideas that stand and have been discussed in space also have far-reaching economic consequences, and we as responsible politicians must always take account of all these decisions, which are now necessary, because wrong measures can also lead to the fact that discharge steps either do not arrive at people or even influence inflation, and that would be the wrong way. We must take a very precise look at the impact the measures have on the employment situation on which groups of people, on which income groups, but also on the economy as a whole. This is the task of a responsible policy. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] As a decision-maker, I believe that it is also good at all levels if we stick to the experts, and we do so very intensively. We are in incredibly close exchange with economic researchers both at national and international level. It is our duty to listen to the experts as well, so that the right measures are taken. We always have to ask ourselves: What do they bring directly to people, will the discharge also arrive? This is a very important point. This is not the case in some of the proposals that are coming. What really or perhaps only partially matters? What measures are particularly important in the near future? The speed is very important in these challenging times. And also: What measures do we need structurally? One is short-term measures, short-term support, and the other is structural reforms. When it comes to these questions, we are very keenly listening to the economic researchers, to the experts, because it is important to make fact-based policies and not perhaps to choose what sounds popular or even populist. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] In the fight against inflation, from our point of view, we need a balanced mix of short-term measures, fast-pacing, fast-pacing measures – these are, in particular, one-off payments, of course, which need to be made quickly to people – and on the other hand, structural measures that work in the medium and long term. Compared with other European countries, I mentioned this earlier, makes us really sure. The first two anti-estimation packages with 4 billion euros were brought to the ground very quickly, but because the situation is still another, it is important to take this next step, with the 28 billion euros that are now being set in motion. This third package is a mixture of emergency aid, emergency measures, rapid one-off payments, but also of structural measures which, at this time, I think, are very important. What is added – Mr Klubobmann has mentioned it – is the eco-social tax reform, which is effective. It is 18 billion euros heavy and now affects people directly, arriving directly at people. These are, of course, tax reliefs for families, and this is crucial because the families suffer intensively from these pressures. Austria is also one of the few countries in Europe which, despite these difficult times, has undergone a tax reform. Many other European countries have cancelled tax reforms due to the situation. We have gone through them because people have to be relieved because the economy has to be relieved. I think that was a real step. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Back to the Anti-Treaty Packages: How are we going? – In a first step, which has already been decided, immediate assistance comes: These are more than 6 billion euros made available to those who are particularly affected, i.e. for minimum pensioners, minimum pensioners, for families. It needs to be quickly helped now in the summer, and we do so in this first step. In the second step, it goes into the width. The medium-sized group is also naturally affected by the pressures. We focused in particular on the income groups between EUR 1 100 and EUR 2 000 gross. They were perhaps too little thought of by the first packages. That's exactly what we're going to do now. The third stage of this process – from my point of view, if you think medium to long-term, is almost the most crucial point, including for the future – includes these structural measures, which are not yet available for decision-making, but which will be reviewed next week, in particular with regard to cold progression. I think this is an important step that we are still doing this before the summer, talking about how the design can work in detail. We have also looked closely at this with the cold progression, the most diverse models available in Europe. The difference with Switzerland, the Swiss model, is that the Swiss only abolished the cold progression on the federal charges, but most of the taxes in the cantons are taxed in the cantons; in the cantons it was abolished in some, in most not. The difference to the German model, where the cold progression has also been abolished to 100 percent, is that in the Germans, 100 percent decide how to give it back. I think our path is a way of understanding: that we make two-thirds of us automated and still have room for a third to distribute socially. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] That is mandatory, that is required by law, that must be returned. That's why: yes, 100 percent abolition of cold progression, but with social compensation. I think that speaks for itself, that is fair and that is also politics with house mind. With the abolition of the cold progression and also with the valorization of social benefits, we also take advantage of the scope. Yes, that's true, we have less room as a government in the future, that's clear, you have to be so fair, but it's not our job, especially in these times, to reject us and to think of us: The additional tax revenue we receive each year through the cold progression, which we then distribute. – It is currently no time for convenience. It is the time for fairness, yes, and that is why such structural measures are urgently needed, and I am glad that we as the Federal Government have finally managed this after 40 years of discussion. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Overall, from my point of view, with this third anti-price package, we have achieved a very balanced package, a very fair package, especially a perfectly secure package. With these measures we help in a first step those people who need it especially, and then we also change the structures towards more fairness. 28 billion euros – yes, that's very, very much money, but we want to give people back what the cost is. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e9829	We'll get to the 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Himmelbauer is speaking on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. – You have the floor, Mrs Deputies.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e9857	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Estimated initiators of popular desire purchase regionally as well as all supporters! We are dealing here with a popular desire, and it is up to the initiators to compensate for the competitive disadvantages of regional businesses compared to online trade by the proposed measures. First of all, I should like to thank you, on behalf of my group, for the personal commitment that the initiators have shown to strengthen regional businesses. As was seen in the very intensive and appreciative debate in the Committee, strengthening the local economy is a concern to all of us, across parties, as well as the concern to purchase regionally to secure jobs in order to secure value creation on the ground, to protect the environment and climate, and, I think, to make a useful contribution to maintaining security of supply. However, I also have to say: With regard to the proposed measures, we need to differentiate. The first point, the taxation of multinational digital companies, is also a matter for us. Austria is one of the leaders who have already introduced and decided to introduce a digital tax in 2019. At the time, we have made a strong signal towards the negotiators at European and OECD level and, as is known, we have already achieved positive steps. At OECD level, on the one hand, a minimum tax rate of 15 percent has been decided, which is to prevent a shift in tax liability in more favourable countries. 130 countries have agreed on this, and I believe that this is a very important and meaningful step. On the other hand, the distribution of tax rights between the State of residence, i.e. the State where the company – an Amazon, a Facebook et cetera – is at home, and the market state, i.e. the State where this company also achieves profits, is being negotiated. In doing so, we also create fiscal justice that taxes are paid and transferred where profits are generated. However, the digital tax is only one point and many other points need to be made in this area. The business models on the Internet are of course very different. There are also good and positive examples from Austria, I call Airbnb's business model, with which you can rent your own residential space via the booking platform. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Matznetter.]] There are already good approaches to be taken, of course, to Europe and worldwide. Another point of popular desire is a lower turnover tax for established trade. – We have invited experts to the Committee’s debate and, among other things, tax experts have pointed out to the Committee section head Mayr that this is not possible under European law. But even if it were possible, I would also like to stress that a distinction between stationary and online trade is also anti-innovation and digitisation. It is precisely the opportunity to offer its services and products online that offers an opportunity to build up an economic existence. I bring the example of a small soap manufacture, which could not build up existence at its production site, if only it had a stationary trade there, because simply the sale would not be given; but because of the possibilities to market online, Austria-wide, Europe-wide, worldwide, this is possible. That is why we support, through the Ministry of Economic Affairs, initiatives to drive digitalisation, e.g. by means of digital SMEs, and we also support the steps at European level. The Digital Markets Act and the Digital Services Act are instruments to ensure fair conditions on the major platforms such as Amazon, where local SMEs also offer, so that these gatekeepers also comply with European rules. Clearly, as a public hand, we can do much more to support the domestic and regional economy. We also do this through other support instruments, for example in the area of local services, especially in the regions or through EU leader projects. I see opportunities for expansion in particular in regional procurement. Minister Tanner has exemplaryly shown that in the barracks we can also rely more on regional procurement, and this would certainly be possible in many other public institutions, for example in judicial institutions. A second point I would like to make is the threshold regulation, through which we also support our communities, to buy directly from their suppliers on the spot, and I may ask that we extend this for the future. Finally, thanks to all supporters. One of the initiators in the Committee, in its modest way, meant that nothing could be achieved by itself – I clearly disagree with that: With our personal purchase decision, we contribute significantly to whether or not local businesses are bought or not, and we contribute to whether on-site shops are purchased and thus existing assets are secured. We have to do it, too. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e12710	Madam President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen. Appreciate guests in the house! We today decide to terminate this agreement with Hungary, as we have done with some countries before. In total, there were twelve states with which Austria had an agreement of this kind. We must take these agreements back on the basis of a decision of the European Court of Justice, because these trade agreements have been re-established throughout Europe. Therefore, these bilateral contracts are no longer valid. That means to us: If a company invests abroad in a neighbouring country, it has received additional protection through this agreement. Thus, a company was able to complain directly and directly in the event of infringements of the company. This can no longer be done without this agreement, it would have to go back to Austria. We are therefore trying to reorganise and strengthen this issue from our country, including with regard to the recasting of the European Union in these trade relations, so that our businesses will also have legal certainty in the future. We know that this has made our ministers responsible excellent in the past. As we have to end these current trade agreements, we have also started bilateral talks with companies immediately so that transitional solutions can be found and they are not suddenly in the legal area, but also with the European Union, we have started negotiations with the European Commission so that trade agreements between all European countries can be concluded in a similar way. Today's decision is therefore a formal act to which I can only ask you for your consent. Thank you.

We are now creating the legal basis for a specialist in occupational medicine. The problem situation or the condition for this is relatively simple: In recent years Few occupational physicians are trained – this is the challenge that we need to present ourselves – but at the same time, the Workers' Protection Act rightly provides mandatory tasks for occupational physicians in the prevention service. From today's point of view, it is therefore foreseeable that the doctors alone will no longer be able to cope with this, and as a result, we have to think of something for the next weeks, months and years. That's how we've created this occupational medical department. In other words, certain health professionals can take over tasks of occupational physicians with an additional training in the establishments, but under the guidance of these occupational physicians and also under their supervision. For this to happen, we need our own quality training, as has already been said. This model is quite praised by many occupational physicians with whom we have talked, and is also considered to be a forward-looking model. What you might want to say about it is: It happened, of course, in agreement with the trade unions, that we started this process and made it, that we have created this occupational medical specialist service. So, as if the whole would only happen at the expense of the workers, from our point of view it is wrong. As a result, I would expect – even in view of the fact that we recognise the competence of non-medical staff members in the health care sector, and I would admit to them these competences, which they have in any case, an approval from the SPÖ colleagues and from these: Trade union involvement, non-medical staff is thereby upgraded. – I think, ladies and gentlemen of SPÖ, it would be good for you to agree to this pragmatic and good solution. It is about these people being able to retain their legal status as frontier workers, even if they have more than 25 percent home office days. As social security issues have to be resolved with neighbouring countries, tax issues have to be resolved with neighbouring countries. For example, in the double taxation agreement with Germany, there is a 45-day limit, and from these 45-days onwards, the German company in Austria will create an establishment, if the employee works too long in the home office. On 31.5., i.e. the day before, the average daily price for diesel was 2.04 euros. ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e23824	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, Values Advisors! Minister! Mrs. Minister! Whether to play a sound file. Transport, environment or health – there is great potential in the public sector datasets. The easier we make access to this public data, the better you can use and exploit this potential. We need to understand Open Government and Open Data as an innovation driver. This strengthens local developers and benefits especially small and medium-sized companies as well as start-ups. With this amendment, we are adapting the IWG, in short, to the new EU directive on open data. In concrete terms, we are dealing with the reduction of obstacles to the re-use of publicly available data. But what is the IWG? – The IWG defines rules for the re-use and processing of datasets provided by public authorities, such as ministries, and also what data can be published in what form. The scope of the IWG is significantly expanded with the current novel and now also collects documents owned by certain public enterprises, such as gas, postal, public transport and aviation sectors. In addition, certain documents are recorded in the possession of researchers as well as research institutions whose production has been publicly funded. These data can usually be used free of charge by developers both commercially and non-commercially to create practical tools for everyday life. Currently, there are also about 700 applications that access and base on these open administrative data. For example, there are web pages that use the data of the Vienna environmental measuring stations and show a practical overview of the pollution pollution in the districts. In addition, traffic data such as the Vienna lines are used for various apps in order to inform users comfortably and easily about waiting times. We all know this: You walk to the subway station and the subway takes you away from your nose. In order not to do so, you can use apps that use these public data and make the way to the metro station efficient. [[As a result of the fact that the European Parliament has not yet taken part in the debate, the Commission has decided to refer the matter to the Court of Justice of the European ……]] So we see: We all benefit from the open and simple availability of public data. That is why I am all the more pleased that we are also developing the IWG with this novelle according to the principle of conceptual and standard open. This will create a further step on the way to an open-government culture. We also agreed on this in the government programme. We want not a glass citizen, but a state and an administration that is glass and transparent. Of course, the protection of personal data remains a high priority. Thus, the IWG does not apply to documents containing personal data if their re-use is not legally compatible with data protection law. One point I would particularly like to highlight has not yet been mentioned, namely the introduction of open data agents who sit in each ministry and will ensure that the data that will be published has a quality that can then be used further. In order to ensure that they can carry out these tasks well, we have equipped them with the resources they need to do the best possible work. Obstacles were also reduced at technical level. Thus, there are no longer any time limits on access or limited numbers of access that have existed in the past and have hindered the further processing of this data. Summary: With the innovation of the IWG, we make it easier and easier to access open data and use it for their projects in Austria. At the same time, we are creating more transparency in the administration. Users, developers and interested citizens benefit from this. I therefore call for the approval of this amendment. - Thank you.

Point Report of the Committee on Research, Innovation and Digitalisation on the Government template (1443, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the E-Government Act (1636 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e23926	We're coming to 19. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Himmelbauer, you are speaking.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e23954	Madam President. Federal Minister! Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! We are now treating a novel of the eGovernment law. Maybe a short introduction: What is the eGovernment law? – It is the legal basis for e-government instruments and applications in Austria. It is admittedly a very technical law, but all the more essential to be able to implement much of what we already have in Austria in eGovernment applications – USP, financial online and much more. The eGovernment Act also regulates, among other things, Supplementary Registers, one of them for non-natural persons or other affected persons. Even if it does not sound that way, natural persons have also been led in it, provided they are entrepreneurship active. Thus, during the first phase of the Covid-19 hardness fund, it was seen that this supplemental register, which has been added to include individual identification numbers in the application, also contains personal data such as the date of birth and address of authorised agents of these companies. This has, of course, raised data protection issues and has also been critically discussed. The then Minister Schramböck immediately removed this supplemental register from the public, and since then a very high-ranking task force has also undertaken to further develop the Supplementary Register, to answer these data protection issues and to plan how we can deal with them in the future. This novel is now the result of this: The Supplementary Register will be reduced to its extent; the groups, which are mainly entrepreneurship active, will also be kept in the business register in the future, and other groups, which can also be kept in other registers, will also. In fact, there are only special cases left in this register, such as public law agencies and foreign companies, and of course no personal data will be provided, provided they do not necessarily have to be apparent. So much was said about this novel. If we talk about eGovernment today, I would also like to take the opportunity to welcome the Secretary of State, who was last present in the Committee on Research, Innovation and Digitalization. Digitalisation is a very further area, and we know that every ministry is in truth responsible for it and should also take action in its field of application. However, I am all the more pleased that in the future there will also be a clause on which the Secretary of State can bring these areas together in the ministries and with which we can also seek synergies. He has already used the last days and weeks since his fishing observation to talk with important stakeholders in Austria, and we will take further steps to further develop the eGovernment in Austria, implement new fields of application and also give citizens new access to their administrative channels. I think we shouldn't do it very badly. There are already many applications that have been implemented in Austria – as I can say a lot to my colleague, Erasim, – such as the Digital Baby Point and the applicationless family support. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Erasim.]] There are countless opportunities such as the USP, Finanzonline et cetera, where we as Austria have taken a pioneering role – and of course we want to continue this path and continue to work there. In this respect, the Secretary of State has already drawn a clear picture in recent weeks. Thank you very much for being able to decide on these novels together. I look forward to the good cooperation.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e24075	Next speaker: Mrs Elisabeth Götze. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e24096	Thank you, Madam President. Values Members of the Federal Government! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It has already been said that we are talking about a novel of the eGovernment law. To explain the background: E-Government means that, in order for public authorities to function digitally, every person wishing to use it needs a clearly-allocated number. These are usually found in the Central Reporting Register, but not all people are reported. This applies, for example, to foreign Austrians, and for this there is a supplementary register for natural persons. The same is true in the business sector, i.e. for entities that are not listed in the company's book – as companies are normally listed – or in the association register. All who are not listed there, but who are economically active in Austria and have legal issues, are included in this Supplement Register. This applies, for example, to churches, to churches, to working communities, but also to free servants or people who receive a lease. In May 2020, there was a data scandal because data from this entrepreneurship supplemental register became public, so it was accessible, and because suddenly people with address were also listed, which was not planned. A Task Force has drawn up seven recommendations on how to fix this gap, and they are now being implemented. Of course, it is important that these data protection issues are solved, i.e. that the Supplementary Registers are not publicly available, but can also function at the same time as simplifications, so that companies are automatically reported when they are active in tax. It is also very important from our point of view to automatically delete the data, either because it is no longer needed or after 30 years, so that automatically cleaning takes place, so that no data cemetery is created. Finally, I would like to say: E-Government really works very well in Austria. You take over this, I think, well ordered house if you want to. Austria is in the roof region, that is, in the German-speaking region, in the first place. There is an eGovernment monitor that always checks how the satisfaction is the use and so on. We're really good at it. We are also among the top five internationally, for example, with regard to transparency and other important indicators. That is why, I believe, we can be proud of it. There's always something to improve, and that's what happens today. Thank you.

We do this in the electricity sector with the Renewable Extension Act, which we do in the heat sector with the Renewable Heat Act, we do so in the transport area with the adaptation of the NoVA, with the largest railway package of all time and so on and so on. However, we must do this within a short period of time. We need to get out of the Russian gas faster than we can drive up the renewables. The reasons for this are: On the one hand, we do not want to be blackmailed by Putin, so that we have to use security policy in front of him for energy policy reasons, and on the other hand, we want to reduce our vulnerability – including that of our industry – with short-term gas supply stops. On the one hand, we do this by means of storage, which goes quickly, and on the other hand, through the Gas Diversification Act. There are also a few other measures, but the Gas Diversification Act is being modernised today, which supports measures with a sum of 100 million euros – and a little beyond that today. There is a regulatory authority which allows the Minister to spend additional sums in order to enable the transfer to other funding countries, Norway and so on, and to replace or grant State support to the costs associated with the use of these fuels. Finally, I would like to make another amendment to which we are concerned that we can also support preparatory steps, for example in the procurement of alternative fuels, in order to promote this diversification. ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e26578	Mr President. Under the title of these two agenda items, a very important issue will be dealt with, namely: Flood protection along the Danube, quite specifically the open sections to improve flood protection, designed for a century high water. Many of you may still have the pictures from 2002 – just 20 years ago – or 2013 in memory, when large-scale floods along the Danube caused huge damage. With today's agenda items on the 15a agreement between the federal states of Upper Austria, Lower Austria and Vienna, this gap is, as it were, closed. By 2030, this flood protection will ultimately be finalized with a total of 222 million euros. This is the light; in the Danube with a large catchment area, it makes sense to protect these areas and above all the settlements. In truth, however, the next question is: What do climate change and changed flood and rain events mean? Precipitation patterns change: What does that mean for the hinterland, for the feeders to the Danube? There, too, the weather patterns, the precipitation patterns have changed significantly. These changes mean that we are now talking about small-space severe precipitation events, so-called crash floods: This is a new concept that has been created in recent years. In fact, we are no longer talking about normal precipitation events, but about small cells, which in recent years have increasingly become extreme amounts of water, strong munitions, especially in alpine areas, denunciations, and – a new phenomenon associated with the rise of the freezing limit in the alpine regions – have also led to completely new quantities of tools. This too will be very busy for us in the near future. What's that? – An appeal to the spatial order in the countries that ultimately regulates whether adequate conservation rooms are created, whether the settlement areas can be protected accordingly, however, which means that the settlement development, the land sealing, the redeployment of land is much more accurate, to be much more economical in order to maintain areas for the preservation of floodwaters. We discussed the energy crisis and climate change earlier: It will not save us if we are always discussing the last point, namely repairing, we need not only to become more economical in soil consumption, but of course also in terms of energy consumption. The climate targets will only be achieved if, at the same time, we are realistically discussing a gradual decline in total energy consumption. To believe that we can continue growth at the expense of natural areas is, of course, unthinkable, and we also have to face this truth.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e27813	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Values Advisors! High house! The 33. StVO-Novelle invites you to look back a little bit. The StVO in its original version dates back to 1960, so it is 62 years old. If you have been observing the development since then, you have to say that it is actually quite pleasing – in the sense that this law was originally totally tailored to the car, to which motorized individual traffic was tailored. Over the years with the many novels – as I said, today we treat the 33. – more and more measures have been taken to show that humanity has recognized that it makes little sense to clog the local cores with cars in particular, that it is important to return the public road surfaces to pedestrians. In the course of the years the pedestrian zone was introduced in § 76a, then the residential street and later the meeting zone was introduced, and today we decide § 76d, the school street. This, in addition to the reforms in the sense of a bicycle-friendly STVO, is an important element of this 33. Novelle. These reforms are, of course, welcomed by us. There is also, of course, an example in many of America, which, of course, does not mean that one should not criticize. I am very, very concerned about the current state of American politics. In “Foreign Policy” it was only recently possible to read a successful analysis that assumes that this country may move so far apart that in the next few years it will be difficult to put it together again. Of course, a Trump would benefit if it broke up, but we would suffer extremely. It would be very difficult for Europe because I believe that we need this partner, the United States, especially in this debate in which we are in the middle of it, namely the struggle of the free, democratic, rule of law against the dictatorships that threaten us with war. Of course, the next thought about the future is just as important, and I have already spoken about it today: Of course, we will have to help rebuild Ukraine. By the way, I also said this in Moldova, where I thanked for Austrian help: Austria is not where we stand today if we had not been helped, and today is the hour in which we need to help not only keep our society together, which is also important to me, but also the European, but above all those of the free states, the states that believe in democracy and the rule of law. In this sense, thank you once again for the idea to George Marshall and to all those who have spread it in Europe. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-06-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00167_d8e30756	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Yes, it has a huge advantage to be on such an evening last speaker. You look in crazyly many confident faces looking forward to the near future. It's nice that everyone's here. I said it to my colleague earlier: I refer to myself personally, then you also have much more joy in speaking. I am pleased with all those involved in the debate; I have noticed that a group has actually not taken part in the foreign policy debate today. It is a very essential issue: 75 years Marshall Plan – we celebrate a symbol, which is a symbolic value of hope, solidarity and compassion. We are therefore celebrating it, as my colleagues have said, because we owe this Marshall plan to the prosperity of Europe at the time. That is why this application is important as part of a strengthened partnership between Austria and the USA and enhanced cooperation between the Diplomatic Academy and the Fulbright Commission. 75 years ago George Marshall, U.S. Foreign Minister, proposed this as an aid program for the first time. At that time, 13 billion dollars were invested, equivalent to 140 billion dollars today. These have been very selflessly invested in supplies, and from these proceeds the destroyed Europe has been rebuilt. Today we have the same question as at the time. There came many and said: On what reservations? What do they want? Surely the pursuits of unhigher goals, that is not just unself-helpful. Today, 75 years later, we repeat exactly the same discussion, the relationship Europe: USA, not only Austria: USA, the relationship between Europe and the world. If we look at Russia Today or Telegram, there is an abrupt assumption that no selflessness could exist. It is even claimed that the Nato would threaten the global security structure, and that is why it is clear and important that we find clear words. Jörg Leichtfried, you talked about enthusiasm earlier. I think it's important that we already highlight: The American Constitution can continue to provide enthusiasm. What we may not like is how it is spent today, but the Constitution may provide enthusiasm. We must also say thank you, because in the last century, the United States has repeatedly brought us out of crises, in which we have perhaps been manoeuvring ourselves as Europe, out of terrible crises, they have stood as a strong partner on our side, but they have been significantly involved in ending World War II. Now, when we think back to the Allies, the occupying forces, what the parent generation says: I have never heard a bad word about the U.S. occupation powers; quite the contrary to many others. This is a recent story: Balkan war ended. Why the US is celebrated as a liberator in Kosovo has the reason that Europe has intervened too late because we were far too undecided. And in the current war of attack on Ukraine – my colleague Brandstätter has just said this – one sees again a very strong role of the USA, because they are often far ahead of us in their determination. No one has prevented us from building up something, building up troops, being self-evident. No one has prevented us today in the field of energy policy, in the security field. This is our own fault, and we might have had to act much earlier. That's supposed to be a warning for us now. Perhaps we should think about it and become proactive ourselves. One question we have to ask ourselves: Who as Europe is our most honest partner out there in the world? Who is most likely to share our values, our values, such as freedom, such as democracy, like the rule of law? There is only one answer to this: We need to maintain transatlantic relations at a high level as Europe, we need to expand them in the economic field, in the cultural field, especially in the security field, in the environmental field. For me, 75 years Marshall Plan means a story of hope and solidarity, but it is also a history of freedom rights. I join Reinhold Lopatka: It's about a free world and it's about unity in this free world. I wish you all a good night and I am glad to see you all again tomorrow. Thank you.
